<a id="q-1"></a>

### Question 1

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Access methods` | **ID:** `q0001`

> **Scenario:**  
> AWS allows users to manage their resources using a web based user interface. What is the name of this interface?

#### Options
- **A.** AWS CLI.
- **B.** AWS API.
- **C.** AWS SDK.
- **D.** AWS Management Console. **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `D`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: AWS Management Console. AWS Management Console — the browser-based interface for managing AWS resources, best suited to exploration rather than repeatable automation. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Access methods.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS CLI — a command line tool for controlling AWS services and scripting repeatable operations.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option B.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS SDK — language-specific libraries that let application code call AWS APIs directly.
- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS Management Console — the browser-based interface for managing AWS resources, best suited to exploration rather than repeatable automation.

---

<a id="q-2"></a>

### Question 2

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Compute` | **ID:** `q0002`

> **Scenario:**  
> Which of the following is an example of horizontal scaling in the AWS Cloud?

#### Options
- **A.** Replacing an existing EC2 instance with a larger, more powerful one.
- **B.** Increasing the compute capacity of a single EC2 instance to address the growing demands of an application.
- **C.** Adding more RAM capacity to an EC2 instance.
- **D.** Adding more EC2 instances of the same size to handle an increase in traffic. **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `D`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Adding more EC2 instances of the same size to handle an increase in traffic. Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Compute.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option A.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option B.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option C.
- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime.

---

<a id="q-3"></a>

### Question 3

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Elasticity` | **ID:** `q0004`

> **Scenario *(Select 2)*:**  
> Which of the below options are related to the reliability of AWS? (Choose TWO)

#### Options
- **A.** Applying the principle of least privilege to all AWS resources.
- **B.** Automatically provisioning new resources to meet demand. **[CORRECT]**
- **C.** All AWS services are considered Global Services, and this design helps customers serve their international users.
- **D.** Providing compensation to customers if issues occur.
- **E.** Ability to recover quickly from failures. **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `B, E`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Automatically provisioning new resources to meet demand; Ability to recover quickly from failures. Auto Scaling — adds and removes EC2 capacity automatically in response to demand, which is how elasticity is implemented in practice. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Elasticity.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Least privilege — granting only the permissions a task actually requires and reviewing them regularly, so a compromised credential has a limited blast radius.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Auto Scaling — adds and removes EC2 capacity automatically in response to demand, which is how elasticity is implemented in practice.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option C.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option D.
- **Option E** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct.

---

<a id="q-4"></a>

### Question 4

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Data migration` | **ID:** `q0008`

> **Scenario *(Select 2)*:**  
> What does AWS Snowball provide? (Choose TWO)

#### Options
- **A.** Built-in computing capabilities that allow customers to process data locally. **[CORRECT]**
- **B.** A catalog of third-party software solutions that customers need to build solutions and run their businesses.
- **C.** A hybrid cloud storage between on-premises environments and the AWS Cloud.
- **D.** An Exabyte-scale data transfer service that allows you to move extremely large amounts of data to AWS.
- **E.** Secure transfer of large amounts of data into and out of the AWS. **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `A, E`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Built-in computing capabilities that allow customers to process data locally; Secure transfer of large amounts of data into and out of the AWS. AWS Snowball — ruggedised physical devices shipped to your site to transfer petabyte-scale data when the network would take too long. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Data migration.

- **Option A** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS Snowball — ruggedised physical devices shipped to your site to transfer petabyte-scale data when the network would take too long.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Marketplace — a curated catalogue of third-party software that can be purchased and deployed with the charges appearing on the AWS bill.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Hybrid cloud — connecting on-premises infrastructure to AWS so the two operate as one environment, usually over Direct Connect or a VPN.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option D.
- **Option E** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct.

<a id="q-5"></a>

### Question 5

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Compute` | **ID:** `q0010`

> **Scenario:**  
> A Japanese company hosts their applications on Amazon EC2 instances in the Tokyo Region. The company has opened new branches in the United States, and the US users are complaining of high latency. What can the company do to reduce latency for the users in the US while minimizing costs?

#### Options
- **A.** Applying the Amazon Connect latency-based routing policy.
- **B.** Registering a new US domain name to serve the users in the US.
- **C.** Building a new data center in the US and implementing a hybrid model.
- **D.** Deploying new Amazon EC2 instances in a Region located in the US. **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `D`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Deploying new Amazon EC2 instances in a Region located in the US. Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Compute.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon Connect — a cloud contact centre that can be set up without telephony hardware.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option B.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option C.
- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime.

<a id="q-6"></a>

### Question 6

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Migration` | **ID:** `q0012`

> **Scenario:**  
> A company has decided to migrate its Oracle database to AWS. Which AWS service can help achieve this without negatively impacting the functionality of the source database?

#### Options
- **A.** AWS OpsWorks.
- **B.** AWS Database Migration Service. **[CORRECT]**
- **C.** AWS Server Migration Service.
- **D.** AWS Application Discovery Service.

**Correct Answer:** `B`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: AWS Database Migration Service. AWS Database Migration Service — migrates databases into AWS with minimal downtime, including between different engines. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Migration.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS OpsWorks — managed instances of the Chef and Puppet configuration management platforms.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS Database Migration Service — migrates databases into AWS with minimal downtime, including between different engines.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Application Migration Service — automates lift and shift migration by replicating on-premises servers into AWS.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option D.

### Question 7

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Block storage` | **ID:** `q0018`

> **Scenario *(Select 2)*:**  
> What should you do in order to keep the data on EBS volumes safe? (Choose TWO)

#### Options
- **A.** Regularly update firmware on EBS devices.
- **B.** Create EBS snapshots. **[CORRECT]**
- **C.** Ensure that EBS data is encrypted at rest. **[CORRECT]**
- **D.** Store a backup daily in an external drive.
- **E.** Prevent any unauthorized access to AWS data centers.

**Correct Answer:** `B, C`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Create EBS snapshots; Ensure that EBS data is encrypted at rest. Amazon EBS — network-attached block volumes for a single EC2 instance, behaving like a virtual hard disk that persists independently of the instance. Encryption at rest — protects stored data so that reading the underlying disk or object yields ciphertext, normally using keys held in AWS KMS. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Block storage.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option A.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon EBS — network-attached block volumes for a single EC2 instance, behaving like a virtual hard disk that persists independently of the instance.
- **Option C** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Encryption at rest — protects stored data so that reading the underlying disk or object yields ciphertext, normally using keys held in AWS KMS.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option D.
- **Option E** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option E.

<a id="q-8"></a>

### Question 8

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Monitoring` | **ID:** `q0020`

> **Scenario *(Select 2)*:**  
> A startup company is operating on limited funds and is extremely concerned about cost overruns. Which of the below options can be used to notify the company when their monthly AWS bill exceeds $2000? (Choose TWO)

#### Options
- **A.** Setup a CloudWatch billing alarm that triggers an SNS notification when the threshold is exceeded. **[CORRECT]**
- **B.** Configure the Amazon Simple Email Service to send billing alerts to their email address on a daily basis.
- **C.** Configure the AWS Budgets Service to alert the company when the threshold is exceeded. **[CORRECT]**
- **D.** Configure AWS CloudTrail to automatically delete all AWS resources when the threshold is exceeded.
- **E.** Configure the Amazon Connect Service to alert the company when the threshold is exceeded.

**Correct Answer:** `A, C`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Setup a CloudWatch billing alarm that triggers an SNS notification when the threshold is exceeded; Configure the AWS Budgets Service to alert the company when the threshold is exceeded. Amazon CloudWatch — collects metrics, logs and alarms describing how resources and applications are performing, and can trigger actions when a threshold is breached. AWS Budgets — sets a cost or usage threshold and alerts you when actual or forecast spend is about to exceed it, which is the proactive counterpart to Cost Explorer. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Monitoring.

- **Option A** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon CloudWatch — collects metrics, logs and alarms describing how resources and applications are performing, and can trigger actions when a threshold is breached.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon SES — sends transactional and marketing email at scale.
- **Option C** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS Budgets — sets a cost or usage threshold and alerts you when actual or forecast spend is about to exceed it, which is the proactive counterpart to Cost Explorer.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS CloudTrail — records every API call in the account including who made it, from which IP address and when, which makes it the service that answers who did this.
- **Option E** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon Connect — a cloud contact centre that can be set up without telephony hardware.

### Question 9

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Content delivery` | **ID:** `q0021`

> **Scenario:**  
> What does Amazon CloudFront use to distribute content to global users with low latency?

#### Options
- **A.** AWS Global Accelerator.
- **B.** AWS Regions.
- **C.** AWS Edge Locations. **[CORRECT]**
- **D.** AWS Availability Zones.

**Correct Answer:** `C`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: AWS Edge Locations. Amazon CloudFront — a content delivery network that caches content at edge locations worldwide to cut latency and offload the origin. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Content delivery.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Global Accelerator — routes user traffic onto the AWS global network at the nearest edge location and provides static anycast IP addresses.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Region — a geographic area containing multiple isolated Availability Zones, chosen based on compliance, latency, service availability and cost.
- **Option C** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon CloudFront — a content delivery network that caches content at edge locations worldwide to cut latency and offload the origin.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Availability Zone — one or more discrete data centres inside a Region with independent power and networking, so deploying across two of them removes a single point of failure.

<a id="q-13"></a>

### Question 13

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Compute` | **ID:** `q0029`

> **Scenario:**  
> You are working on a project that involves creating thumbnails of millions of images. Consistent uptime is not an issue, and continuous processing is not required. Which EC2 buying option would be the most cost-effective?

#### Options
- **A.** Reserved Instances.
- **B.** On-demand Instances.
- **C.** Dedicated Instances.
- **D.** Spot Instances. **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `D`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> The answer is Spot Instances. Read the wording carefully, because this question asks for the option that does not belong. Every other option is a true statement. Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Compute.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Reserved Instances — a one or three year commitment to a specific instance configuration in exchange for a large discount over On-Demand pricing.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): On-Demand Instances — pay by the second with no commitment, best for unpredictable workloads and short-term testing.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Dedicated Hosts — a physical server allocated to a single customer, usually chosen to satisfy per-socket software licensing or strict compliance requirements.
- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Spot Instances — spare capacity at up to ninety percent off, which AWS can reclaim with two minutes notice, so it suits fault-tolerant and interruptible work.

### Question 18

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Networking` | **ID:** `q0039`

> **Scenario *(Select 2)*:**  
> In order to implement best practices when dealing with a “Single Point of Failure,” you should attempt to build as much automation as possible in both detecting and reacting to failure. Which of the following AWS services would help? (Choose TWO)

#### Options
- **A.** ELB. **[CORRECT]**
- **B.** Auto Scaling. **[CORRECT]**
- **C.** Amazon Athen.
- **D.** ECR.
- **E.** Amazon EC2.

**Correct Answer:** `A, B`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: ELB; Auto Scaling. Elastic Load Balancing — distributes incoming traffic across healthy targets in multiple Availability Zones and stops sending requests to instances that fail health checks. Auto Scaling — adds and removes EC2 capacity automatically in response to demand, which is how elasticity is implemented in practice. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Networking.

- **Option A** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Elastic Load Balancing — distributes incoming traffic across healthy targets in multiple Availability Zones and stops sending requests to instances that fail health checks.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Auto Scaling — adds and removes EC2 capacity automatically in response to demand, which is how elasticity is implemented in practice.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option C.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon ECR — a private registry for storing, versioning and scanning Docker container images.
- **Option E** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime.

### Question 23

#### Options
- **A.** AWS Elastic Load Balancer.
- **B.** AWS Budgets.
- **C.** AWS Auto Scaling. **[CORRECT]**
- **D.** AWS Cost Explorer.

**Correct Answer:** `C`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: AWS Auto Scaling. Auto Scaling — adds and removes EC2 capacity automatically in response to demand, which is how elasticity is implemented in practice. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Elasticity.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Elastic Load Balancing — distributes incoming traffic across healthy targets in multiple Availability Zones and stops sending requests to instances that fail health checks.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Budgets — sets a cost or usage threshold and alerts you when actual or forecast spend is about to exceed it, which is the proactive counterpart to Cost Explorer.
- **Option C** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Auto Scaling — adds and removes EC2 capacity automatically in response to demand, which is how elasticity is implemented in practice.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Cost Explorer — visualises and analyses historic cost and usage, and forecasts future spend based on observed trends.

- **D.** Deploying your AWS resources across multiple Availability Zones.

- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Elastic Load Balancing — distributes incoming traffic across healthy targets in multiple Availability Zones and stops sending requests to instances that fail health checks.

- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon Lightsail — pre-configured virtual private servers with a simple fixed monthly price, aimed at small workloads and users new to AWS.

<a id="q-60"></a>

### Question 60

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Backup and recovery` | **ID:** `q0120`

> **Scenario:**  
> A company has business critical workloads hosted on AWS and they are unwilling to accept any downtime. Which of the following is a recommended best practice to protect their workloads in the event of an unexpected natural disaster?

#### Options
- **A.** Replicate data across multiple Edge Locations worldwide and use Amazon CloudFront to perform automatic failover in the event of an outage.
- **B.** Deploy AWS resources across multiple Availability Zones within the same AWS Region.
- **C.** Create point-in-time backups in another subnet and recover this data when a disaster occurs.
- **D.** Deploy AWS resources to another AWS Region and implement an Active-Active disaster recovery strategy. **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `D`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Deploy AWS resources to another AWS Region and implement an Active-Active disaster recovery strategy. AWS Elastic Disaster Recovery — continuously replicates servers into AWS so they can be recovered quickly after an outage. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Backup and recovery.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Edge location — a CloudFront cache site placed close to users to reduce latency, far more numerous than Regions.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Availability Zone — one or more discrete data centres inside a Region with independent power and networking, so deploying across two of them removes a single point of failure.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option C.
- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS Elastic Disaster Recovery — continuously replicates servers into AWS so they can be recovered quickly after an outage.

Amazon RDS — managed relational databases where AWS handles provisioning, patching, backups and failover for engines such as MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQL Server. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Relational databases.

- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS Elastic Beanstalk — uploads your application code and automatically provisions the capacity, load balancing, scaling and health monitoring behind it.

- **E.** Amazon EBS.

This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Object storage.

- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon EMR — managed Hadoop, Spark and Presto clusters for large-scale data processing.

Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Compute.

- **Option A** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): True of Amazon EC2, so it is not the option this question is asking for.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): True of Amazon EC2, so it is not the option this question is asking for.

Rather than scaling the server vertically, the company has decided to deploy three Amazon EC2 instances in parallel and to distribute traffic across the three servers. What AWS Service should the company use to distribute the traffic evenly?

#### Options
- **A.** AWS Global Accelerator.
- **B.** AWS Application Load Balancer (ALB). **[CORRECT]**
- **C.** Amazon CloudFront.
- **D.** Transit VPC.

**Correct Answer:** `B`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: AWS Application Load Balancer (ALB). Elastic Load Balancing — distributes incoming traffic across healthy targets in multiple Availability Zones and stops sending requests to instances that fail health checks. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Networking.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Global Accelerator — routes user traffic onto the AWS global network at the nearest edge location and provides static anycast IP addresses.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Elastic Load Balancing — distributes incoming traffic across healthy targets in multiple Availability Zones and stops sending requests to instances that fail health checks.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon CloudFront — a content delivery network that caches content at edge locations worldwide to cut latency and offload the origin.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon VPC — a logically isolated private network in AWS where you define the IP range, subnets, route tables and gateways.

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Operations` | **ID:** `q0233`

> **Scenario:**  
> Which AWS Service provides integration with Chef to automate the configuration of EC2 instances?

#### Options
- **A.** AWS Config.
- **B.** AWS OpsWorks. **[CORRECT]**
- **C.** AutoScaling.
- **D.** AWS CloudFormation.

**Correct Answer:** `B`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: AWS OpsWorks. AWS OpsWorks — managed instances of the Chef and Puppet configuration management platforms. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Operations.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Config — records how resource configurations change over time and evaluates each change against rules to flag non-compliant resources.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS OpsWorks — managed instances of the Chef and Puppet configuration management platforms.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Auto Scaling — adds and removes EC2 capacity automatically in response to demand, which is how elasticity is implemented in practice.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS CloudFormation — provisions and updates infrastructure from declarative JSON or YAML templates so environments can be recreated identically.

<a id="q-111"></a>

### Question 111

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Object storage` | **ID:** `q0234`

> **Scenario:**  
> A customer is seeking to store objects in their AWS environment and to make those objects downloadable over the internet. Which AWS Service can be used to accomplish this?

#### Options
- **A.** Amazon EBS.
- **B.** Amazon EFS.
- **C.** Amazon S3. **[CORRECT]**
- **D.** Amazon Instance Store.

**Correct Answer:** `C`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Amazon S3. Amazon S3 — durable object storage for any amount of unstructured data, with versioning, lifecycle rules and eleven nines of durability. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Object storage.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon EBS — network-attached block volumes for a single EC2 instance, behaving like a virtual hard disk that persists independently of the instance.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon EFS — a managed NFS file system that many EC2 instances across multiple Availability Zones can mount at the same time.
- **Option C** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon S3 — durable object storage for any amount of unstructured data, with versioning, lifecycle rules and eleven nines of durability.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): EC2 instance store — physically attached ephemeral disk that delivers very high IOPS but loses all data when the instance stops or terminates.

- **D.** AWS CloudTrail.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS WAF — filters HTTP and HTTPS requests at the application layer to block patterns such as SQL injection and cross-site scripting before they reach the application.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon CloudWatch — collects metrics, logs and alarms describing how resources and applications are performing, and can trigger actions when a threshold is breached.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Cloud9 — a browser-based integrated development environment with the AWS tooling preinstalled.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS CloudTrail — records every API call in the account including who made it, from which IP address and when, which makes it the service that answers who did this.

### Question 114

- **C.** AWS Artifact.
- **D.** Amazon CloudFront.

**Correct Answer:** `A`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option B.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Artifact — the self-service portal for downloading AWS compliance reports such as SOC and ISO, and for accepting agreements.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon CloudFront — a content delivery network that caches content at edge locations worldwide to cut latency and offload the origin.

### Question 118

This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Serverless compute.

<a id="q-125"></a>

### Question 125

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Compute` | **ID:** `q0259`

> **Scenario:**  
> A company’s AWS workflow requires that it periodically perform large-scale image and video processing jobs. The customer is seeking to minimize cost and has stated that the amount of time it takes to process these jobs is not critical, but that cost minimization is the most important factor in designing the solution. Which EC2 instance class is best suited for this processing?

#### Options
- **A.** EC2 On-Demand Instances.
- **B.** EC2 Reserved Instances     - No Upfront.
- **C.** EC2 Spot Instances. **[CORRECT]**
- **D.** EC2 Reserved Instances     - All Upfront.

**Correct Answer:** `C`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> The answer is EC2 Spot Instances. Read the wording carefully, because this question asks for the option that does not belong. Every other option is a true statement. Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Compute.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): On-Demand Instances — pay by the second with no commitment, best for unpredictable workloads and short-term testing.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Reserved Instances — a one or three year commitment to a specific instance configuration in exchange for a large discount over On-Demand pricing.
- **Option C** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Spot Instances — spare capacity at up to ninety percent off, which AWS can reclaim with two minutes notice, so it suits fault-tolerant and interruptible work.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option D.

- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon S3 Glacier — the lowest-cost S3 storage classes for archives, trading minutes-to-hours retrieval time for a very low price per gigabyte.
- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon S3 — durable object storage for any amount of unstructured data, with versioning, lifecycle rules and eleven nines of durability.

### Question 131

- **C.** You should deploy critical components of your application in the Availability Zone that you trust.
- **D.** You can track all API calls using Amazon Athena.

**Correct Answer:** `B`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: You should regularly patch the operating system and applications on your EC2 instances. Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Compute.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): EC2 instance store — physically attached ephemeral disk that delivers very high IOPS but loses all data when the instance stops or terminates.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Availability Zone — one or more discrete data centres inside a Region with independent power and networking, so deploying across two of them removes a single point of failure.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon Athena — runs standard SQL directly against files in Amazon S3 with no infrastructure to run, billed per terabyte scanned.

### Question 158

- **A.** Amazon Detective.
- **B.** AWS X-Ray. **[CORRECT]**
- **C.** AWS Security Hub.
- **D.** AWS Shield.

**Correct Answer:** `B`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon Detective — builds a linked behaviour graph from logs and findings to help investigate the root cause of a security issue after it is detected.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS X-Ray — traces a request as it moves through a distributed application to show where latency and errors originate.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Security Hub — aggregates and normalises findings from GuardDuty, Inspector, Macie and partner tools into one dashboard and scores them against security standards.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Shield — defends against distributed denial of service attacks, with the Standard tier enabled free for every account and Advanced adding response team access and cost protection.

<a id="q-159"></a>

### Question 159

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Containers` | **ID:** `q0311`

> **Scenario *(Select 2)*:**  
> Which of the following compute resources are serverless? (Choose TWO)

#### Options
- **A.** Amazon EC2.
- **B.** AWS Fargate. **[CORRECT]**
- **C.** AWS Lambda. **[CORRECT]**
- **D.** Amazon ECS.
- **E.** Amazon EMR.

**Correct Answer:** `B, C`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: AWS Fargate; AWS Lambda. AWS Fargate — a serverless compute engine for containers that removes the need to provision or manage the EC2 instances behind ECS and EKS. AWS Lambda — runs code in response to events with no servers to manage, scales automatically, and bills only for the milliseconds the code executes. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Containers.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS Fargate — a serverless compute engine for containers that removes the need to provision or manage the EC2 instances behind ECS and EKS.
- **Option C** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS Lambda — runs code in response to events with no servers to manage, scales automatically, and bills only for the milliseconds the code executes.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon ECS — AWS-native container orchestration that schedules and runs Docker containers across a cluster.
- **Option E** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon EMR — managed Hadoop, Spark and Presto clusters for large-scale data processing.

- **D.** DMS.

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Developer tools` | **ID:** `q0342`

> **Scenario:**  
> Which of the following services is an AWS repository management system that allows for storing, versioning, and managing your application code?

#### Options
- **A.** AWS CodePipeline.
- **B.** AWS CodeCommit. **[CORRECT]**
- **C.** AWS X-Ray.
- **D.** Amazon Inspector.

**Correct Answer:** `B`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: AWS CodeCommit. AWS CodeCommit — private managed Git repositories hosted in AWS. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Developer tools.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS CodePipeline — orchestrates the whole continuous delivery pipeline, chaining source, build, test and deploy stages.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS CodeCommit — private managed Git repositories hosted in AWS.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS X-Ray — traces a request as it moves through a distributed application to show where latency and errors originate.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon Inspector — automatically scans EC2 instances, container images and Lambda functions for known software vulnerabilities and unintended network exposure.

<a id="q-175"></a>

### Question 175

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Networking` | **ID:** `q0343`

> **Scenario:**  
> Which AWS service can be used to route end users to the nearest AWS Region to reduce latency?

#### Options
- **A.** Amazon Cognito.
- **B.** AWS Systems Manager.
- **C.** AWS Cloud9.
- **D.** Amazon Route 53. **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `D`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Amazon Route 53. Amazon Route 53 — a managed DNS service that also registers domains, performs health checks and supports latency, geolocation and failover routing. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Networking.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon Cognito — handles sign-up, sign-in and identity federation for the end users of your web and mobile applications, not for AWS console access.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Systems Manager — provides operational control of instances at scale, including patching, run commands, shell access without open ports, and parameter storage.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Cloud9 — a browser-based integrated development environment with the AWS tooling preinstalled.
- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon Route 53 — a managed DNS service that also registers domains, performs health checks and supports latency, geolocation and failover routing.

### Question 176

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Compute` | **ID:** `q0351`

> **Scenario:**  
> A company wants to use Amazon Elastic Container Service (Amazon ECS) to run its containerized applications. For compliance reasons, the company wants to retain complete visibility and control over the underlying server cluster. Which Amazon ECS launch type will satisfy these requirements?

#### Options
- **A.** EC2 launch type. **[CORRECT]**
- **B.** Fargate launch type.
- **C.** Lightsail launch type.
- **D.** Lambda launch type.

**Correct Answer:** `A`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: EC2 launch type. Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Compute.

- **Option A** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Fargate — a serverless compute engine for containers that removes the need to provision or manage the EC2 instances behind ECS and EKS.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon Lightsail — pre-configured virtual private servers with a simple fixed monthly price, aimed at small workloads and users new to AWS.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Lambda — runs code in response to events with no servers to manage, scales automatically, and bills only for the milliseconds the code executes.

- **A.** AWS manages everything related to EC2 operating systems.

- **C.** Server side encryption is the responsibility of AWS.
- **D.** AWS is responsible for the security of your application.

**Correct Answer:** `B`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: AWS customers are responsible for patching any database software running on Amazon EC2. Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Compute.

- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Encryption at rest — protects stored data so that reading the underlying disk or object yields ciphertext, normally using keys held in AWS KMS.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option D.

<a id="q-179"></a>

### Question 179

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Monitoring` | **ID:** `q0360`

> **Scenario:**  
> A company is running a large web application that needs to always be available. The application tends to slow down when CPU usage is greater than 60%. How can they track when CPU usage goes above 60% for any of the EC2 Instances in their account?

#### Options
- **A.** Use CloudFront to monitor the CPU usage.
- **B.** Set the AWS Config CPU threshold to 60% to receive a notification when EC2 usage exceeds that value.
- **C.** Use CloudWatch Alarms to monitor the CPU and alert when the CPU usage is >= 60%. **[CORRECT]**
- **D.** Use SNS to monitor the utilization of the server.

**Correct Answer:** `C`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Use CloudWatch Alarms to monitor the CPU and alert when the CPU usage is >= 60%. Amazon CloudWatch — collects metrics, logs and alarms describing how resources and applications are performing, and can trigger actions when a threshold is breached. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Monitoring.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon CloudFront — a content delivery network that caches content at edge locations worldwide to cut latency and offload the origin.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Config — records how resource configurations change over time and evaluates each change against rules to flag non-compliant resources.
- **Option C** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon CloudWatch — collects metrics, logs and alarms describing how resources and applications are performing, and can trigger actions when a threshold is breached.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon SNS — a publish and subscribe service that pushes a single message out to many subscribers such as queues, functions, email or SMS.

<a id="q-190"></a>

### Question 190

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Elasticity` | **ID:** `q0384`

> **Scenario *(Select 2)*:**  
> The elasticity of the AWS Cloud enables customers to save costs when compared to traditional hosting providers. What can AWS customers do to benefit from the elasticity of the AWS Cloud? (Choose TWO)

#### Options
- **A.** Deploy your resources across multiple Availability Zones.
- **B.** Use Amazon EC2 Auto Scaling. **[CORRECT]**
- **C.** Deploy your resources in another region.
- **D.** Use Elastic Load Balancing.
- **E.** Use Serverless Computing whenever possible. **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `B, E`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Use Amazon EC2 Auto Scaling; Use Serverless Computing whenever possible. Auto Scaling — adds and removes EC2 capacity automatically in response to demand, which is how elasticity is implemented in practice. Serverless — a model where AWS manages all capacity, patching and scaling, leaving only code and configuration as the customer responsibility. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Elasticity.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Availability Zone — one or more discrete data centres inside a Region with independent power and networking, so deploying across two of them removes a single point of failure.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Auto Scaling — adds and removes EC2 capacity automatically in response to demand, which is how elasticity is implemented in practice.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Region — a geographic area containing multiple isolated Availability Zones, chosen based on compliance, latency, service availability and cost.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Elasticity — acquiring capacity when demand rises and releasing it when demand falls, so cost follows actual load.
- **Option E** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Serverless — a model where AWS manages all capacity, patching and scaling, leaving only code and configuration as the customer responsibility.

---

- **C.** API Gateway.
- **D.** Amazon Direct Connect. **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `A, D`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: AWS VPN; Amazon Direct Connect. AWS Site-to-Site VPN — an encrypted tunnel between an on-premises network and a VPC over the public internet, quick to set up but subject to internet variability. AWS Direct Connect — a dedicated private physical link between a data centre and AWS that bypasses the public internet for consistent bandwidth and latency. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Hybrid networking.

- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon Redshift — a petabyte-scale data warehouse built for complex analytical queries and business intelligence reporting, not for transactional workloads.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon API Gateway — publishes, secures and throttles REST and WebSocket APIs, commonly as the front door to Lambda functions.
- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS Direct Connect — a dedicated private physical link between a data centre and AWS that bypasses the public internet for consistent bandwidth and latency.

When updates or patches are released by OS vendors, AWS Managed Services applies them in a timely and consistent manner to minimize the impact on your business - Critical security patches are applied immediately, while others are applied based on the patch schedule you request. Backups of Stacks are automated using Amazon Elastic Block Store (EBS) and RDS snapshots, and can be restored in the event of a failure or outage, ensuring business continuity This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Relational databases.

<a id="q-256"></a>

### Question 256

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Hybrid networking` | **ID:** `q0567`

> **Scenario *(Select 2)*:**  
> A company wants to migrate its applications to a VPC on AWS These applications will need to access on-premises resources. What combination of actions will enable the company to accomplish this goals? (Select TWO)

#### Options
- **A.** Use the AWS Service Catalog to identify a list of on-premises resources that can be migrated
- **B.** Build a VPN connection between an on-premises device and a virtual private gateway in the new VPC **[CORRECT]**
- **C.** Use Amazon Athena to query data from the on-premises database servers
- **D.** Connect the company’s on-premises data center to AWS using AWS Direct Connect **[CORRECT]**
- **E.** Leverage Amazon CloudFront to restrict access to static web content provided through the company’s on-premises web servers

**Correct Answer:** `B, D`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Build a VPN connection between an on-premises device and a virtual private gateway in the new VPC; Connect the company’s on-premises data center to AWS using AWS Direct Connect. AWS Site-to-Site VPN — an encrypted tunnel between an on-premises network and a VPC over the public internet, quick to set up but subject to internet variability. AWS Direct Connect — a dedicated private physical link between a data centre and AWS that bypasses the public internet for consistent bandwidth and latency. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Hybrid networking.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Service Catalog — lets an organisation publish an approved list of products that users may deploy, keeping deployments within agreed standards.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS Site-to-Site VPN — an encrypted tunnel between an on-premises network and a VPC over the public internet, quick to set up but subject to internet variability.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon Athena — runs standard SQL directly against files in Amazon S3 with no infrastructure to run, billed per terabyte scanned.
- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS Direct Connect — a dedicated private physical link between a data centre and AWS that bypasses the public internet for consistent bandwidth and latency.
- **Option E** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon CloudFront — a content delivery network that caches content at edge locations worldwide to cut latency and offload the origin.

> Which of the following services could be used to deploy an application to servers running on-premises? (Select TWO)

#### Options
- **A.** AWS Elastic Beanstalk.
- **B.** AWS OpsWorks. **[CORRECT]**
- **C.** AWS CodeDeploy. **[CORRECT]**
- **D.** AWS Batch.
- **E.** AWS X-Ray.

**Correct Answer:** `B, C`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: AWS OpsWorks; AWS CodeDeploy. AWS OpsWorks — managed instances of the Chef and Puppet configuration management platforms. AWS CodeDeploy — automates the rollout of application code to EC2, Lambda or ECS with controlled strategies and rollback. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Operations.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Elastic Beanstalk — uploads your application code and automatically provisions the capacity, load balancing, scaling and health monitoring behind it.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS OpsWorks — managed instances of the Chef and Puppet configuration management platforms.
- **Option C** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS CodeDeploy — automates the rollout of application code to EC2, Lambda or ECS with controlled strategies and rollback.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Batch — runs long-running batch computing jobs by provisioning the right amount of compute and queuing work, with no job duration limit.
- **Option E** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS X-Ray — traces a request as it moves through a distributed application to show where latency and errors originate.

- **D.** Amazon EC2 with Amazon Elastic Block Store (Amazon EBS). **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `D`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime.
- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon EBS — network-attached block volumes for a single EC2 instance, behaving like a virtual hard disk that persists independently of the instance.

<a id="q-270"></a>

### Question 270

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Serverless compute` | **ID:** `q0619`

> **Scenario:**  
> How does AWS charge for AWS Lambda?

#### Options
- **A.** Users bid on the maximum price they are willing to pay per hour.
- **B.** Users choose a 1-, 3- or 5-year upfront payment term.
- **C.** Users pay for the required permanent storage on a file system or in a database.
- **D.** Users pay based on the number of requests and consumed compute resources. **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `D`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Users pay based on the number of requests and consumed compute resources. AWS Lambda — runs code in response to events with no servers to manage, scales automatically, and bills only for the milliseconds the code executes. - AWS Lambda is charging its users by the number of requests for their functions and by the duration, which is the time the code needs to execute. - When code starts running in response to an event, AWS Lambda counts a request. - It will charge the total number of requests across all of the functions used. - Duration is calculated by the time when your code started executing until it returns or until it is terminated, rounded up near to 100ms. - The AWS Lambda pricing depends on the amount of memory that the user used to allocate to the function. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Serverless compute.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option A.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option B.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option C.
- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct.

---

<a id="q-271"></a>

### Question 271

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Compute` | **ID:** `q0620`

> **Scenario:**  
> What function do security groups serve related Amazon Elastic Compute Cloud (Amazon EC2) instance security?

#### Options
- **A.** Act as a virtual firewall for the Amazon EC2 instance. **[CORRECT]**
- **B.** Secure AWS user accounts with AWS identity and Access Management (IAM) policies.
- **C.** Provide DDoS protection with AWS Shield.
- **D.** Use Amazon CloudFront to protect the Amazon EC2 instance.

**Correct Answer:** `A`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Act as a virtual firewall for the Amazon EC2 instance. Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime. - AWS Security Groups act like a firewall for your Amazon EC2 instances controlling both inbound and outbound traffic. - When you launch an instance on Amazon EC2, you need to assign it to a particular security group. - After that, you can set up ports and protocols, which remain open for users and computers over the internet. - AWS Security Groups are very flexible. You can use the default security group and still customize it according to your liking (although we don't recommend this practice because groups should be named according to their purpose.) Or you can create a security group that you want for your specific applications. - To do this, you can write the corresponding code or use the Amazon EC2 console to make the process easier. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Compute.

- **Option A** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS IAM — controls who is authenticated and what they are authorised to do, using users, groups, roles and JSON policies, at no additional cost.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Shield — defends against distributed denial of service attacks, with the Standard tier enabled free for every account and Advanced adding response team access and cost protection.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option D.

<a id="q-272"></a>

### Question 272

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Content delivery` | **ID:** `q0624`

> **Scenario:**  
> A company is designing an application hosted in a single AWS Region serving end-users spread across the world. The company wants to provide the end-users low latency access to the application data. <br/> Which of the following services will help fulfill this requirement?

#### Options
- **A.** Amazon CloudFront **[CORRECT]**
- **B.** AWS Direct Connect
- **C.** Amazon Route 53 global DNS
- **D.** Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3) transfer acceleration

**Correct Answer:** `A`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Amazon CloudFront. Amazon CloudFront — a content delivery network that caches content at edge locations worldwide to cut latency and offload the origin. - Amazon CloudFront is a content delivery network (CDN) service that distributes data from multiple locations worldwide, providing low-latency access to end-users. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Content delivery.

- **Option A** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon CloudFront — a content delivery network that caches content at edge locations worldwide to cut latency and offload the origin.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Direct Connect — a dedicated private physical link between a data centre and AWS that bypasses the public internet for consistent bandwidth and latency.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon Route 53 — a managed DNS service that also registers domains, performs health checks and supports latency, geolocation and failover routing.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon S3 — durable object storage for any amount of unstructured data, with versioning, lifecycle rules and eleven nines of durability.

<a id="q-273"></a>

### Question 273

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Elasticity` | **ID:** `q0632`

> **Scenario:**  
> Which feature adds elasticity to Amazon EC2 instances to handle the changing demand for workloads?

#### Options
- **A.** Resource groups
- **B.** Lifecycle policies
- **C.** Application Load Balancer
- **D.** Amazon EC2 Auto Scaling **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `D`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Amazon EC2 Auto Scaling. Auto Scaling — adds and removes EC2 capacity automatically in response to demand, which is how elasticity is implemented in practice. - Support for monitoring the health of each service independently, as health checks are defined at the target group level and many CloudWatch metrics are reported at the target group level. - Attaching a target group to an Auto Scaling group enables you to scale each service dynamically based on demand. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Elasticity.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option A.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option B.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Elastic Load Balancing — distributes incoming traffic across healthy targets in multiple Availability Zones and stops sending requests to instances that fail health checks.
- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Auto Scaling — adds and removes EC2 capacity automatically in response to demand, which is how elasticity is implemented in practice.

<a id="q-274"></a>

### Question 274

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Hybrid storage` | **ID:** `q0634`

> **Scenario:**  
> Which AWS hybrid storage service enables on-premises applications to seamlessly use AWS Cloud storage through standard file-storage protocols?

#### Options
- **A.** AWS Direct Connect
- **B.** AWS Snowball
- **C.** AWS Storage Gateway **[CORRECT]**
- **D.** AWS Snowball Edge

**Correct Answer:** `C`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: AWS Storage Gateway. AWS Storage Gateway — an on-premises appliance that presents local NFS, SMB or iSCSI storage while backing the data into AWS. - The AWS Storage Gateway service enables hybrid cloud storage between on-premises environments and the AWS Cloud. - It seamlessly integrates on-premises enterprise applications and workflows with Amazon's block and object cloud storage services through industry standard storage protocols. - It provides low-latency performance by caching frequently accessed data on premises, while storing data securely and durably in Amazon cloud storage services. - It provides an optimized data transfer mechanism and bandwidth management, which tolerates unreliable networks and minimizes the amount of data being transferred. - It brings the security, manageability, durability, and scalability of AWS to existing enterprise environments through native integration with AWS encryption, identity management, monitoring, and storage services. Typical use cases include backup and archiving, disaster recovery, moving data to S3 for in-cloud workloads, and tiered storage. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Hybrid storage.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Direct Connect — a dedicated private physical link between a data centre and AWS that bypasses the public internet for consistent bandwidth and latency.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Snowball — ruggedised physical devices shipped to your site to transfer petabyte-scale data when the network would take too long.
- **Option C** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS Storage Gateway — an on-premises appliance that presents local NFS, SMB or iSCSI storage while backing the data into AWS.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option D.

---

<a id="q-275"></a>

### Question 275

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Relational databases` | **ID:** `q0636`

> **Scenario:**  
> Which architectural principle is used when deploying an Amazon Relational Database Service (Amazon RDS) instance in Multiple Availability Zone mode?

#### Options
- **A.** Implement loose coupling.
- **B.** Design for failure. **[CORRECT]**
- **C.** Automate everything that can be automated.
- **D.** Use services, not servers.

**Correct Answer:** `B`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Design for failure. Amazon RDS — managed relational databases where AWS handles provisioning, patching, backups and failover for engines such as MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQL Server. - Amazon RDS Multi-AZ deployments provide enhanced availability and durability for Database (DB) Instances, making them a natural fit for production database workloads. - When you provision a Multi-AZ DB Instance, Amazon RDS automatically creates a primary DB Instance and synchronously replicates the data to a standby instance in a different Availability Zone (AZ). - Each AZ runs on its own physically distinct, independent infrastructure, and is engineered to be highly reliable. - In case of an infrastructure failure, Amazon RDS performs an automatic failover to the standby (or to a read replica in the case of Amazon Aurora), so that you can resume database operations as soon as the failover is complete. - Since the endpoint for your DB Instance remains the same after a failover, your application can resume database operation without the need for manual administrative intervention. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Relational databases.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Loose coupling — connecting components through queues or load balancers so the failure of one does not cascade into the others.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option C.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option D.

---

<a id="q-276"></a>

### Question 276

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Hybrid networking` | **ID:** `q0639`

> **Scenario:**  
> A director has been tasked with investigating hybrid cloud architecture. The company currently accesses AWS over the public internet.<br/>Which service will facilitate private hybrid connectivity?

#### Options
- **A.** Amazon Virtual Private Cloud (Amazon VPC) NAT Gateway
- **B.** AWS Direct Connect **[CORRECT]**
- **C.** Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3) Transfer Acceleration
- **D.** AWS Web Application Firewall (AWS WAF)

**Correct Answer:** `B`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: AWS Direct Connect. AWS Direct Connect — a dedicated private physical link between a data centre and AWS that bypasses the public internet for consistent bandwidth and latency. - Amazon VPC provides multiple network connectivity options for you to leverage depending on your current network designs and requirements. - These connectivity options include leveraging either the internet or an AWS Direct Connect connection as the network backbone and terminating the connection into either AWS or user-managed network endpoints. - Additionally, with AWS, you can choose how network routing is delivered between Amazon VPC and your networks, leveraging either AWS or user-managed network equipment and routes. introduction.html This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Hybrid networking.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon VPC — a logically isolated private network in AWS where you define the IP range, subnets, route tables and gateways.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS Direct Connect — a dedicated private physical link between a data centre and AWS that bypasses the public internet for consistent bandwidth and latency.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon S3 — durable object storage for any amount of unstructured data, with versioning, lifecycle rules and eleven nines of durability.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS WAF — filters HTTP and HTTPS requests at the application layer to block patterns such as SQL injection and cross-site scripting before they reach the application.

---

<a id="q-277"></a>

### Question 277

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Block storage` | **ID:** `q0646`

> **Scenario:**  
> Which type of AWS storage is ephemeral and is deleted when an instance is stopped or terminated?

#### Options
- **A.** Amazon EBS
- **B.** Amazon EC2 instance store **[CORRECT]**
- **C.** Amazon EFS
- **D.** Amazon S3

**Correct Answer:** `B`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Amazon EC2 instance store. EC2 instance store — physically attached ephemeral disk that delivers very high IOPS but loses all data when the instance stops or terminates. - When you stop or terminate an instance, every block of storage in the instance store is reset. - Therefore, your data cannot be accessed through the instance store of another instance. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Block storage.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon EBS — network-attached block volumes for a single EC2 instance, behaving like a virtual hard disk that persists independently of the instance.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. EC2 instance store — physically attached ephemeral disk that delivers very high IOPS but loses all data when the instance stops or terminates.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon EFS — a managed NFS file system that many EC2 instances across multiple Availability Zones can mount at the same time.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon S3 — durable object storage for any amount of unstructured data, with versioning, lifecycle rules and eleven nines of durability.

---

<a id="q-278"></a>

### Question 278

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Networking` | **ID:** `q0648`

> **Scenario:**  
> Which of the following is an important architectural principle when designing cloud applications?

#### Options
- **A.** Store data and backups in the same region.
- **B.** Design tightly coupled system components.
- **C.** Avoid multi-threading.
- **D.** Design for failure **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `D`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Design for failure. - There are six design principles for operational excellence in the cloud: - Perform operations as code - Annotate documentation - Make frequent, small, reversible changes - Refine operations procedures frequently - Anticipate failure - Learn from all operational failures This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Networking.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Region — a geographic area containing multiple isolated Availability Zones, chosen based on compliance, latency, service availability and cost.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option B.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option C.
- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct.

---

<a id="q-279"></a>

### Question 279

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Relational databases` | **ID:** `q0650`

> **Scenario:**  
> Which of the following services is a MySQL-compatible database that automatically grows storage as needed?

#### Options
- **A.** Amazon Elastic Compute Cloud (Amazon EC2)
- **B.** Amazon Relational Database Service (Amazon RDS) for MySQL
- **C.** Amazon Lightsail
- **D.** Amazon Aurora **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `D`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Amazon Aurora. Amazon Aurora — an AWS-built database compatible with MySQL and PostgreSQL that delivers much higher throughput and replicates storage across three Availability Zones. - Amazon Aurora is a relational database service that combines the speed and availability of high-end commercial databases with the simplicity and cost-effectiveness of open source databases. - The MySQL- compatible edition of Aurora delivers up to 5X the throughput of standard MySQL running on the same hardware, and enables existing MySQL applications and tools to run without requiring modification. - Amazon Aurora will automatically grow the size of your database volume as your database storage needs grow. - Your volume will grow in increments of 10 GB up to a maximum of 64 TB. You don't need to provision excess storage for your database to handle future growth. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Relational databases.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon RDS — managed relational databases where AWS handles provisioning, patching, backups and failover for engines such as MySQL, PostgreSQL and SQL Server.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon Lightsail — pre-configured virtual private servers with a simple fixed monthly price, aimed at small workloads and users new to AWS.
- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon Aurora — an AWS-built database compatible with MySQL and PostgreSQL that delivers much higher throughput and replicates storage across three Availability Zones.

<a id="q-280"></a>

### Question 280

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Networking` | **ID:** `q0651`

> **Scenario:**  
> Which Amazon Virtual Private Cloud (Amazon VPC) feature enables users to connect two VPCs together?

#### Options
- **A.** Amazon VPC endpoints
- **B.** Amazon Elastic Compute Cloud (Amazon EC2) ClassicLink
- **C.** Amazon VPC peering **[CORRECT]**
- **D.** AWS Direct Connect

**Correct Answer:** `C`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Amazon VPC peering. VPC peering — a private one-to-one network link between two VPCs, which does not support transitive routing. - A VPC peering connection is a networking connection between two VPCs that enables you to route traffic between them using private IPv4 addresses or IPv6 addresses. - Instances in either VPC can communicate with each other as if they are within the same network. - You can create a VPC peering connection between your own VPCs, or with a VPC in another AWS account. - The VPCs can be in different regions (also known as an inter-region VPC peering connection). This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Networking.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): VPC endpoint — lets resources in a VPC reach AWS services over the AWS private network instead of traversing the internet.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime.
- **Option C** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. VPC peering — a private one-to-one network link between two VPCs, which does not support transitive routing.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Direct Connect — a dedicated private physical link between a data centre and AWS that bypasses the public internet for consistent bandwidth and latency.

---

<a id="q-281"></a>

### Question 281

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Data migration` | **ID:** `q0653`

> **Scenario:**  
> Which AWS service provides a secure, fast, and cost-effective way to migrate or transport exabyte-scale datasets into AWS?

#### Options
- **A.** AWS Batch
- **B.** AWS Snowball
- **C.** AWS Migration Hub
- **D.** AWS Snowmobile **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `D`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: AWS Snowmobile. AWS Snowball — ruggedised physical devices shipped to your site to transfer petabyte-scale data when the network would take too long. - AWS Snowmobile is an exabyte-scale data transfer service that can move extremely large amounts of data to AWS in a fast, secure, and cost-effective manner. - You can transfer up to 100PB per Snowmobile, a 45-foot long ruggedized shipping container, pulled by a semi-trailer truck. - Snowmobile makes it easy to move massive volumes of data to the cloud, including video libraries, image repositories, or even a complete data center migration. - All data is encrypted with 256-bit encryption and you can manage your encryption keys with AWS Key Management Service (AWS KMS). - Snowmobile includes GPS tracking, alarm monitoring, 24/7 video surveillance and an optional escort security vehicle while in transit. aws-snowmobile/ This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Data migration.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Batch — runs long-running batch computing jobs by provisioning the right amount of compute and queuing work, with no job duration limit.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option B.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Migration Hub — tracks the progress of application migrations across multiple AWS and partner migration tools in one place.
- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. AWS Snowball — ruggedised physical devices shipped to your site to transfer petabyte-scale data when the network would take too long.

---

<a id="q-282"></a>

### Question 282

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Networking` | **ID:** `q0655`

> **Scenario *(Select 2)*:**  
> Which load balancer types are available with Elastic Load Balancing (ELB)? (Choose two.)

#### Options
- **A.** Public load balancers with AWS Application Auto Scaling capabilities
- **B.** F5 Big-IP and Citrix NetScaler load balancers
- **C.** Classic Load Balancers **[CORRECT]**
- **D.** Cross-zone load balancers with public and private IPs
- **E.** Application Load Balancers **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `C, E`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Classic Load Balancers; Application Load Balancers. Elastic Load Balancing — distributes incoming traffic across healthy targets in multiple Availability Zones and stops sending requests to instances that fail health checks. - Elastic Load Balancing supports the following types of load balancers: Application Load Balancers, Network Load Balancers, and Classic Load Balancers. - Amazon ECS services can use either type of load balancer. - Application Load Balancers are used to route HTTP/HTTPS (or Layer 7) traffic. - Network Load Balancers and Classic Load Balancers are used to route TCP (or Layer 4) traffic. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Networking.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option A.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option B.
- **Option C** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Elastic Load Balancing — distributes incoming traffic across healthy targets in multiple Availability Zones and stops sending requests to instances that fail health checks.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option D.
- **Option E** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct.

### Question 283

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Compute` | **ID:** `q0659`

> **Scenario:**  
> Which of the following AWS services can be used to run a self-managed database?

#### Options
- **A.** Amazon Route 53
- **B.** AWS X-Ray
- **C.** AWS Snowmobile
- **D.** Amazon Elastic Compute Cloud (Amazon EC2) **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `D`

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon Route 53 — a managed DNS service that also registers domains, performs health checks and supports latency, geolocation and failover routing.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS X-Ray — traces a request as it moves through a distributed application to show where latency and errors originate.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Snowball — ruggedised physical devices shipped to your site to transfer petabyte-scale data when the network would take too long.
- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime.

<a id="q-284"></a>

### Question 284

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Monitoring` | **ID:** `q0670`

> **Scenario:**  
> Which AWS service can be used to generate alerts based on an estimated monthly bill?

#### Options
- **A.** AWS Config
- **B.** Amazon CloudWatch **[CORRECT]**
- **C.** AWS X-Ray
- **D.** AWS CloudTrail

**Correct Answer:** `B`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Amazon CloudWatch. Amazon CloudWatch — collects metrics, logs and alarms describing how resources and applications are performing, and can trigger actions when a threshold is breached. - You can monitor your estimated AWS charges by using Amazon CloudWatch. - When you enable the monitoring of estimated charges for your AWS account, the estimated charges are calculated and sent several times daily to CloudWatch as metric data. - Billing metric data is stored in the US East (N. Virginia) Region and represents worldwide charges. - This data includes the estimated charges for every service in AWS that you use, in addition to the estimated overall total of your AWS charges. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Monitoring.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Config — records how resource configurations change over time and evaluates each change against rules to flag non-compliant resources.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon CloudWatch — collects metrics, logs and alarms describing how resources and applications are performing, and can trigger actions when a threshold is breached.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS X-Ray — traces a request as it moves through a distributed application to show where latency and errors originate.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS CloudTrail — records every API call in the account including who made it, from which IP address and when, which makes it the service that answers who did this.

<a id="q-285"></a>

### Question 285

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Storage` | **ID:** `q0671`

> **Scenario:**  
> Which of the following is the responsibility of AWS?

#### Options
- **A.** Setting up AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) users and groups
- **B.** Physically destroying storage media at end of life **[CORRECT]**
- **C.** Patching guest operating systems
- **D.** Configuring security settings on Amazon EC2 instances

**Correct Answer:** `B`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Physically destroying storage media at end of life. - Media storage devices used to store customer data are classified by AWS as Critical and treated accordingly, as high impact, throughout their life-cycles. - AWS has exacting standards on how to install, service, and eventually destroy the devices when they are no longer useful. - When a storage device has reached the end of its useful life, AWS decommissions media using techniques detailed in NIST 800-88. - Media that stored customer data is not removed from AWS control until it has been securely decommissioned. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Storage.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS IAM — controls who is authenticated and what they are authorised to do, using users, groups, roles and JSON policies, at no additional cost.
- **Option B** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Evaluation of option C.
- **Option D** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon EC2 — resizable virtual machines where you choose the instance type, control the guest operating system, and pay per second or hour of runtime.

---

<a id="q-286"></a>

### Question 286

**Domain:** `Domain 3: Cloud Technology and Services` | **Topic:** `Content delivery` | **ID:** `q0673`

> **Scenario:**  
> Which AWS service would a customer use with a static website to achieve lower latency and high transfer speeds?

#### Options
- **A.** AWS Lambda
- **B.** Amazon DynamoDB Accelerator
- **C.** Amazon Route 53
- **D.** Amazon CloudFront **[CORRECT]**

**Correct Answer:** `D`

#### Comprehensive Explanations

> Correct answer: Amazon CloudFront. Amazon CloudFront — a content delivery network that caches content at edge locations worldwide to cut latency and offload the origin. - Amazon CloudFront is a fast content delivery network (CDN) service that securely delivers data, videos, applications, and APIs to customers globally with low latency, high transfer speeds, all within a developer- friendly environment. - CloudFront is integrated with AWS both physical locations that are directly connected to the AWS global infrastructure, as well as other AWS services. This item belongs to the Cloud Technology and Services domain, under Content delivery.

- **Option A** ❌ (INCORRECT): AWS Lambda — runs code in response to events with no servers to manage, scales automatically, and bills only for the milliseconds the code executes.
- **Option B** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon DynamoDB — a serverless key-value NoSQL database with single-digit millisecond latency at any scale and no servers to manage.
- **Option C** ❌ (INCORRECT): Amazon Route 53 — a managed DNS service that also registers domains, performs health checks and supports latency, geolocation and failover routing.
- **Option D** ✅ (CORRECT): Correct. Amazon CloudFront — a content delivery network that caches content at edge locations worldwide to cut latency and offload the origin.
